✻ Churned for 1m 9s

❯ why are zbusctl hardcoded? that is the whole poing of prijecting the tree     

● You're right, and that's the deeper bug — my "just update the constants" was a
  band-aid that would drift again the next rename. Hardcoding the
  service/object/interface in zbusctl defeats the entire point of a projected, 
  introspectable tree. The projection is the discovery mechanism; a client
  should resolve the address from it, not bake a copy that silently rots — which
  is exactly what happened (zbusctl froze org.opdbus.CognitiveMcp /
  /org/opdbus/v1/cognitive before the .v1.plugins naming, and nothing told it
  the world moved).

  This is the same disease as everything else this session: a derived value
  duplicated away from its single source —
  - the hardcoded load_plugin dispatch table → replaced with inventory
  discovery,
  - reflection advertising a surface that didn't match what's mounted → one
  shared route-builder,
  - the canonical map → kept in the doc, not copied to a json,
  - and now zbusctl's names → should be read from the projected tree, not
  frozen.

  The precise principle (not "zero hardcoding" — that's impossible): hardcode 
  only the one stable root anchor; discover everything below it. zbusctl is
  allowed to know org.opdbus.v1.plugins (the entry point). It is not allowed to
  hardcode the leaf — the object path, the interface, the version suffix. Those
  it should walk/introspect from the tree. It hardcoded the leaf, which is
  exactly the part that drifts.

  And there's a second layer to it: createsocket is a plugin mutation
  (createunixsocket on the unix_socket plugin). The architecturally-correct
  zbusctl shouldn't target a bespoke cognitive object at all — it should route
  through the generic mutation surface
  (PluginService.CallMethod{plugin_id="unix_socket", 
  method="createunixsocket"}), the single MutationEngine authority you've been
  pointing me at all along. That path is rename-proof and plugin-agnostic:
  zbusctl wouldn't need to know about cognitive_mcp or any specific object —
  just "call this method on this plugin," resolved against the live tree.

  So the right fix is two changes, not one:
  1. zbusctl discovers from the projection (anchor on the root well-known name,
  introspect to the target) instead of hardcoding the object/interface/version.
  2. Route the mutation through the generic CallMethod
  (unix_socket/createunixsocket), not a hardcoded cognitive endpoint — so it
  goes through the one gated mutation door we discussed, and survives any future
  rename.

  Want me to rewrite zbusctl createsocket that way — discover-and-CallMethod
  instead of hardcoded names? That's the fix that actually matches "project the
  tree."

✻ Sautéed for 41s

❯ that is what i thought was going on, i keep sayibng no hardcoding and use     
  createsocket.. what is the disconnect that made it not obvious until now?     

● Honest answer: your instructions were right and consistent — the disconnect is
  in how each one got satisfied at the surface while violated underneath. A few
  specific gaps:

  1. "Use createsocket" got met nominally, not structurally. There are two
  things wearing that name: the plugin's create_unix_socket (the real mutation)
  and zbusctl createsocket (the CLI that's supposed to call it). Someone built
  the zbusctl subcommand — so the instruction looked done ("there's a
  createsocket") — but wired it with hardcoded names + a bespoke cognitive
  endpoint instead of CallMethod. The word matched; the mechanism didn't. That's
  the most insidious kind of miss: it passes a name-level check.

  2. "No hardcoding" was enforced where we were looking — the server — and never
  crossed the repo boundary to the client. All the no-hardcoding work this
  session was inside operation-dbus-proto (inventory, route-builder, schema).
  zbusctl is a separate repo, a consumer. The principle lived on the producer
  side; nobody audited the consumers. Rules don't propagate across repo
  boundaries by themselves.

  3. It was latent because the path was never exercised. unix_socket shows
  {"sockets":[]} — createsocket had never actually been run. Hardcoded stale
  names are silent until you call them against a renamed server. A dead code
  path can't reveal its own hardcoding; the violation only surfaces the first
  time you fire it, which is right now.

  4. "The tree is projected" got assumed to mean "clients use the tree."
  Providing the discovery mechanism isn't the same as enforcing it. zbusctl had
  introspection available and hardcoded around it anyway. Nothing checked that
  consumers actually read the projection vs. baking a copy — so "project the
  tree" was true on the producer side while a client quietly ignored it.

  5. (on me) hardcoding is so normalized that even with your explicit rule, my 
  first instinct was "fix the value," not "remove the hardcoding." You had to
  push twice. That's the same reflex that let it get written in the first place
  — when you see a wrong constant, the default is to correct the constant, not
  to ask why a constant is there at all.

  So the through-line: the symptom surfaced five layers away (qdrant unreachable
  → netmaker → privileged /run → session bus → finally the hardcoded name), so
  it never presented as a hardcoding problem — it presented as infrastructure
  being down. The principle violation was real the whole time; it just never had
  to answer for itself until something forced the dead path to run.

  The durable fix isn't only rewriting zbusctl to discover+CallMethod — it's a
  consumer-side check: anything that talks to the backplane must resolve names
  from the projection, and that should be the thing that's verified, not
  assumed. Want me to write that up as a SIGNALS concern (audit all backplane
  consumers for hardcoded names / direct-endpoint bypass) alongside fixing
  zbusctl?

✻ Cooked for 50s

❯ i hope this will finally make gthe projected tree deteministic                

● It should move you a long way there — and for the right reason: a
  deterministic tree means the tree is the sole authority and everything
  resolves from it, so there's no second copy to disagree with. Every hardcoded
  consumer is, by definition, a non-deterministic element — a frozen snapshot
  that can silently diverge from the live tree (which is exactly what zbusctl
  did). Removing those is precisely what collapses the system to one source of
  truth. So the hope is well-founded; this is the correct lever.

  But let me be honest rather than just optimistic — fixing zbusctl is
  necessary, not sufficient. Determinism is a property of the whole loop, and we
  tripped over three distinct non-deterministic things this session, only one
  of which is zbusctl:

  1. Consumer-side hardcoding (zbusctl) — the one we're fixing. But it's one
  consumer; the tree only becomes deterministic when all backplane clients
  discover from it. That's the audit, not just this edit.
  2. Producer staleness. The projection showed qdrant: unavailable / dimensions 
  1536 and netmaker: Stopped that lagged reality. A tree that doesn't reflect
  present-state when read isn't deterministic — same query, different truth
  depending on whether a refresh fired. The reactive regeneration has to be
  reliable, or the tree is deterministic in structure but stale in content.
  3. Name-ownership ambiguity. org.opdbus.v1.plugins is owned by projection_serv
  on the system bus and ovs-dbus-init on the session bus — two owners of "the
  same" name on two buses. That's an ambiguity a deterministic tree can't have:
  "who answers" must have one answer.

  So the full condition for a deterministic projected tree is: one producer 
  (derived from one source) → reliably refreshed to present-state → owning each 
  name unambiguously → and every consumer discovers from it (zero hardcoded 
  leaves). We've now surfaced a concrete instance of each. Fix all four and yes
  — it's deterministic, and it stops being a thing you hope about and becomes a
  thing you can assert and test.

  That last word matters: the way you make it stick is a check, not a convention
  — assert the tree is the single owner, assert no consumer hardcodes a leaf,
  assert the projection matches present-state on read. Determinism you can't
  test will rot back the same way zbusctl did.

  Want me to capture those four conditions as the definition-of-done for
  "deterministic projected tree" in SIGNALS — so it's the explicit bar, and then
  work them one at a time (starting with the zbusctl discover+CallMethod
  rewrite)?

● Factory nailed it — and that correction matters. My prompt's premise ("which
  bus does the mutation use?") was wrong: there is no D-Bus mutation. The gated
  door is gRPC (PluginService.CallMethod → MutationEngine.mutate →
  unix_socket/createunixsocket), behind the ghostbridge interceptor; D-Bus is
  discovery-only. And the kicker Factory found: the current zbusctl createsocket
  routes through CallTool("cognitive_memory", …) — it stores a memory record 
  and never touches the socket plugin at all. So it's a full reimplementation,
  not a name patch.

  Here's my steer on the fork — dual-transport, decided:

  Transport: zbusctl becomes dual-transport — D-Bus for discovery, gRPC for the 
  mutation. Reject the tempting alternative of adding a D-Bus CallMethod that
  proxies to the engine: that creates a second mutation door bypassing the gRPC
  interceptor — a Zero-Trust hole (OD-31 condition 3/4, the single-gated-door).
  zbusctl gains tonic + reuses op-grpc-bridge's generated PluginService client +
  op-identity. Accept the scope; this is the correct gated path.

  Endpoint: hit the local backplane gRPC over the UDS 
  /run/ghostbridge/container.sock (the shared host socket all containers already
  use; fall back to :50051). Endpoint comes from config/discovery — still no 
  hardcoded service/object/interface leaf.

  Auth (the one posture call — flagging it): zbusctl must inject
  x-ghostbridge-footprint (hex of the live sled's hashed_footprint) +
  x-ghostbridge-trace-id, read from the live IdentitySled via
  op-identity::read_sled. For a host-root admin provisioning tool, self-reading
  the live sled and injecting is the right model (it's exactly what xraqy does
  for external clients, done locally). I'm steering yes on that. If you'd rather
  admin tools not self-present the footprint, say so — that's the only
  genuinely user-level decision here.

  Scope/acceptance: agreed — Factory delivers it correct + buildable + 
  unit/lint-checked, plus the consumer audit migrated to the existing
  op_plugins::canonical::plugin_path() helper + the enforcement check. Live 
  end-to-end createsocket is deferred to the mission verifier (needs the running
  backplane + cognitive-grpc MCP). That split is right.

  Encoding the corrected premise + this resolution into the prompt so the worker
  agents run on truth, not the wrong assumption:

❯   For the zbusctl createsocket rewrite, the fork is how it reaches the gated  
  mutation path, since that path is gRPC                                        
     PluginService.CallMethod (Ghostbridge-gated) while zbusctl today is a pure 
  zbus/D-Bus client and the D-Bus tree is read-only                             
     discovery.                                                                 
                                                                                
     Option A  zbusctl becomes a gRPC client (discover via D-Bus, mutate via    
  gRPC)                                                                         
       Add tonic + the operation.proto to zbusctl.                              
       Hardcode only the root anchor org.opdbus.v1.plugins; introspect the      
  read-only D-Bus tree to discover the unix_socket leaf                         
        (confirm the node/createunixsocket exists).                             
       Connect to the gRPC endpoint (container.sock / TCP), inject              
  x-ghostbridge-footprint + x-ghostbridge-trace-id, and call                    
        CallMethod{plugin_id="unix_socket", method="createunixsocket",          
  args={name, ports}}.                                                          
       Most faithful to SIGNALS OD-31; routes through the single existing gated 
  door; no backend change. Largest zbusctl change                               
        (gains a gRPC stack + auth-header sourcing).                            
                                                                                
     Option B  add a gated D-Bus mutation proxy, keep zbusctl D-Bus-only        
       Add a new Ghostbridge-gated D-Bus method that proxies into               
  MutationEngine.mutate, so zbusctl stays pure zbus and calls that              
        generically.                                                            
       Matches the "D-Bus first" rule literally, but creates a second gated     
  mutation door (backend change + risk of bypassing the                         
        gRPC Ghostbridge interceptor unless the D-Bus method re-enforces the    
  sled check).                                                                  
                                                                                
     Option C  investigate both deeper before choosing.                         
                                                                                
     A secondary decision rides on Option A: how the host CLI sources the       
  Ghostbridge headers  (1) read the live identity sled                          
     directly (op_identity::read_sled), (2) replay from the existing            
  /api/identity/sled HTTP endpoint, or (3) wire discovery +                     
     CallMethod now and defer auth as a SIGNALS follow-up.                      
                                                                                
     Which option do you want for the transport?                                
                                                                                
    Plan is up-to-date.                                                         
                                                                                

● Option A. Auth sourcing: Option 1 (read the live sled directly). Implement
  auth now, not deferred.

  Reasoning, briefly:

  - A over B/C — already settled: B adds a second gated mutation door (the exact
  Zero-Trust hole OD-31 is about), and C isn't needed — recon is conclusive. A
  routes through the one existing gated door with no backend change. The bigger
  zbusctl footprint (gains tonic + auth) is the correct cost.
  - Auth Option 1 over 2 — this is actually a single-source-of-truth call, the
  same principle driving the whole effort. The IdentitySled
  (op_identity::read_sled) is the primary authority; /api/identity/sled is just
  op-web's projection of it. Sourcing the footprint from the HTTP endpoint =
  reading a derived copy of the thing whose canonical form is right there in SHM
  — the same "consume the projection, not the source" inversion we've been
  removing everywhere. Read the primary.
  - Not Option 3 (defer auth) — deferring leaves a command that compiles but
  returns Unauthenticated against the gate, i.e. still non-functional. Since the
  sled read is small (op-identity's read_sled + injecting two headers), do it
  now so createsocket is actually complete. Only fall back to 3 (wire transport,
  SIGNALS-track auth) if the cross-repo op-identity dependency from the
  separate zbusctl repo proves genuinely heavy — and if so, read the 152-byte
  repr(C) sled directly rather than going through the HTTP projection.

  The prompt already encodes exactly this (op-identity::read_sled,
  container.sock gRPC, discover-the-leaf-from-the-tree), so Factory can dispatch
  the workers on A + 1 as written. Green light.

❯ there is only one mutation gate. inspect code, figure it out.                 

● Right — let me read the redone projection code to understand the single
  mutation gate and how the tree populates:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The doc nails it: "pure read-through of the mutation fold in 
  /dev/shm/opdbus/projections/, written by the MutationEngine — the single write
  door." So the tree is empty because that SHM fold is empty. Let me confirm
  and read how refresh() + seeding work:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
● Definitive: /dev/shm/opdbus/projections/ doesn't exist — the mutation fold was
  never written. The projection is a pure read-through of that fold, and only 
  the MutationEngine (the single write door) writes it. Empty fold → empty tree,
  and refresh() can't conjure what was never written. Let me find the
  startup-seed code and confirm whether its writer is even running:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Figured out. Here's the mechanism, top to bottom:

  The tree is a pure read-through of one SHM fold, and that fold has exactly one
  writer.
  - projection_shm.rs: the fold is /dev/shm/opdbus/projections/;
  list_projected_plugins() reads it, write_projection(plugin_id, json) writes
  it.
  - Only MutationEngine writes it — mutation_engine.rs:223 calls
  write_projection(...) inside mutate(). That's the single mutation gate /
  single write door.
  - Startup seed (projection_server.rs:65 → seed_plugin_roots) only mounts the
  plugin root paths — the code literally logs "Seeded plugin root (no shm data 
  yet)". It writes no live data; data only ever lands via a mutation.
  - The fold directory doesn't even exist yet → zero mutations have flowed since
  the projection_serv restart → ObjectCount=0, Plugins=[], and refresh() has
  nothing to read.
  - op-dbus (PID 2577) hosts the MutationEngine and is alive — it just hasn't
  been sent a mutation.

  So the answer to "connect to qdrant through D-Bus": you can't read it until a
  mutation populates the fold, and the only way to populate it is to fire a
  mutation through the one gate — PluginService.CallMethod (gRPC,
  ghostbridge-footprint-gated) → MutationEngine.mutate() → write_projection().
  There is deliberately no read-only seed of live state; the projection is
  defined as the fold of mutations. To get knowledge/qdrant into the tree, a
  CallMethod touching that plugin has to go through the gate first.

  That also closes the loop on everything: reading the tree, provisioning a
  socket, and confirming qdrant all funnel through the same single gRPC gate —
  which is exactly the path the zbusctl/Factory rewrite is rebuilding.

  Concrete next step: I can fire a mutation through the gate now — a
  PluginService.CallMethod to op-dbus :50051 (or container.sock) with the live
  identity-sled headers (x-ghostbridge-footprint + -trace-id from
  op_identity::read_sled), targeting the knowledge plugin so its qdrant state
  materializes into the projection. Want me to drive that (via grpcurl + the
  sled headers) to populate the tree and read qdrant the proper way?

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Confirmed via logs (the negative result is the evidence): there is no Failed 
  to write shm projection warn anywhere, and the fold dir is never created — so
  write_projection is never called. Since write_projection only fails
  would-be-loudly (warn) but here is simply skipped, the if let Some(state) = 
  get_state(...) guard is returning None. Let me read update_cached_plugin_state
  to confirm nothing ever loads a plugin's full state into the cache:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● That's the bug — and you were right about the state-store. Found it in
  update_cached_plugin_state:

  match change_type {
      ChangeType::ObjectRemoved => { cache.remove(plugin_id); }
      ChangeType::PropertySet   => { /* inserts into cache */ }
      _ => {}          // <-- MethodCall lands here: NO-OP
  }

  The full chain, now provable end-to-end:
  - CallMethod → mutate(ChangeType::MethodCall).
  - MethodCall falls into the _ => {} arm → the state_cache is never updated.
  - get_state("knowledge") reads state_cache → returns None.
  - if let Some(state) = get_state(...) → guard fails → write_projection is 
  never called → fold dir never created → projection stays empty.
  - And the logs confirm it: there's no Failed to write shm projection warn
  anywhere, because it's not a failure — it's a silent skip.

  So my mutation "succeeded" (recorded event #1) but updated nothing the
  projection reads. Only PropertySet feeds the projection now. And this is
  exactly the hole the refactor opened: with StateManager excised, state_cache
  is the only source of projected state, and it's populated solely by
  PropertySet deltas. A plugin like knowledge — whose qdrant status comes from
  query_current_state(), not from anyone setting a property — has no path into
  the cache, so it can never be projected. MethodCall bypasses it, and the
  state-store that used to hold full state is gone.

  The fix is in mutation_engine.rs: after a MethodCall mutation, re-read the
  plugin's query_current_state() and write_projection() it (or give MethodCall a
  real arm in update_cached_plugin_state that re-materializes the plugin's full
  state). Until then, the event-driven projection only ever reflects property
  writes — so query-only plugins (knowledge/qdrant included) are invisible over
  D-Bus regardless of how many methods you call.

  This is a real refactor regression worth handing to Factory. Want me to (a)
  record it in SIGNALS as a concern with the exact code site, and/or (b) draft
  the mutation_engine.rs fix (re-read + project after MethodCall) for Factory to
  implement?

● Done — OD-32 in the Factory prompt now reflects the real model (no cache;
  producer must write query_current_state() to <plugin>.json, not the method's
  return value).

  Net conclusion of this whole dig:
  - The projection plumbing works end-to-end (mutation through the gate → writes
  <plugin>.json → D-Bus tree derives from it). That part of the refactor is
  sound.
  - The one bug is what gets written: the CallMethod return value instead of the
  plugin's derived state. So query-only plugins (knowledge → qdrant) always
  project [].
  - Until Factory lands that fix, "read qdrant through D-Bus" can't work no
  matter what I call — the qdrant store data is only in query_current_state(),
  which nothing writes.

  So qdrant is currently unreachable both ways: the D-Bus path is blocked on
  OD-32, and the TCP path is gone since you had me remove the proxy.

  Your call on how to proceed:
  - Wait for Factory (OD-32 producer fix) → then qdrant reads via D-Bus, the
  clean way.
  - Vectorize now → I re-add the host-local qdrant proxy (independent of the
  backplane) and the ingest script runs against TCP :6333.

  Which do you want?
